[9/17/26, 10:53:04 PM] +234 818 554 6555: The METI UniPods AI Innovation Programme - Cohort 1 Chatbot Hackathon is a challenge to solve a real problem we all face in the group. It is like a side hustle. Not a major reason why we are here. 

The Problem:
Chat volume is too high; people miss messages and ask the same questions again. People also miss meetings and can't rewatch all the recordings, so information gets lost.

The Challenge:
Build a working AI chatbot that can read chats + call recordings and answer people's questions directly.

How to go about it:
1. Form a team TODAY:
- Up to 5 people
- Members cannot all be from the same country
- Must include at least one woman
- Deadline: Close of business TODAY, Thursday 17 Sept 2026

To declare your team, the Team Lead should send an email with:
Subject: UniPods Hackathon
Body: Team Name + Names of members + their countries

2. Build Phase:
- Friday 18 Sept to Thursday 24 Sept
- You must submit: A working chatbot + source code link + short setup notes on how to run it.

3. Prize & Judging:
- $5,000 cash prize
- Your solution will actually be deployed to solve the problem
- Judging will be done by the whole group.

It's not a slide deck competition - they want a working bot that solves the FAQ/catchup problem.

Do you have a team already? If not, you should quickly join an incomplete team here.

[9/18/26, 8:52:30 AM] +229 01 49 48 62 56: 🌙 Daily Recap — 17 September

The day was mostly about the hackathon: lots of energy went into forming teams before the deadline (introductions, finding teammates, balancing gender and nationalities). Several teams came together.

📚 Wadhwani session (Module 1 — Problem Statement)
Charles had everyone work on their problem statement: start with who experiences the problem, what it is and the root cause — never the solution. Keep it to one short paragraph (350 characters, then 150 to sharpen it). Separate your primary and secondary customer, and think about who will pay. Personal written feedback comes at Milestone 1 (~3 weeks). The recording is available.

🎥 UN video-demo opportunity
- A 5-minute slot to present your solution at the UN General Assembly event ("Building the Workforce of the Future", 20 Sep, New York).
- Deadline: Friday 18 Sep, 2:00 PM CAT. Video 5 min max (shorter is fine).
- Show your solution in action (one clear use case), your story, your impact, your UniPods/METI journey.
- Technical: landscape, 1080p, good sound, well-lit, English preferred, MP4 file.
- Submit: name the file Country_SolutionName_YourName, upload to Drive/WeTransfer (set to "anyone with the link"), then email unipods.regional@undp.org with your name, country, UniPod, solution name, a one-line description, email and phone.

📢 Other announcements
- Open Hours: optional 1-hour sessions to ask questions specific to your own project/business or the challenges you're facing, and to talk about the support you need (not for general Wadhwani/MIT questions). First "Ask Us Anything" on Friday at 3:00 PM CAT, then every Monday (with Gift) and Wednesday (with Diane).
- Several members earned their MIT "Introduction to Universal AI" certificate 🎉
- Hackathon team declaration: email unipods.regional@undp.org, subject "UniPods Hackathon – Team Declaration".

The course is self-paced and covers the foundations of AI, including generative AI, machine learning, prescriptive AI, Python and data analysis. The 16 foundational modules are compulsory, while the industry-specific vertical modules are optional.

Only one person per solution/team is enrolled, and the certificate will be issued in that person’s name. The course is expected to be completed by 18 October 2026 to remain eligible for the next stage of the programme.

You can access the course through the individual invitation link sent by MIT. After signing in, open Dashboard to see the modules. The recording is available here:

[9/18/26, 9:39:16 AM] +221 77 147 42 67: Hello what mean UN demo video please ?
[9/18/26, 9:39:29 AM] +229 01 49 48 62 56: The UN demo video is a short video opportunity for one METI AI innovator to be showcased during the UN General Assembly event in New York on 20 September.

Your team can submit a video of up to 5 minutes introducing yourselves and demonstrating your working AI solution. It should show the problem, your solution in action, your progress or impact, and your vision.

To submit it:

1. Name the file: Country_SolutionName_YourName
2. Upload it to Google Drive or WeTransfer and allow “anyone with the link can view”.
3. Email the link to unipods.regional@undp.org with your full name, country, UniPod, solution name, one-line description, email and phone number.

The deadline is today, Friday 18 September at 2:00 PM CAT. The video should be landscape, preferably MP4, with clear sound. Avoid using only slides—show the working solution if possible.

The programme has four main parts: MIT Universal AI for foundational AI skills; Wadhwani Ignite for building a viable business around your AI solution; the Ethiopian AI Institute for intermediate-to-advanced AI training; and an in-person AI bootcamp in Addis Ababa for selected teams. The strongest teams may later receive additional support, including possible funding and links to timbuktoo Hubs.

Where we are now: MIT Universal AI is open and self-paced, with expected completion by 18 October. Wadhwani Ignite is in Week 1 of 14. The current focus is understanding and clearly defining your problem — who experiences it, what the problem is, and its root cause, without describing the solution.

Today, Friday 18 September, the hackathon starts. The hackathon deliverables are a working chatbot, source-code/repository access, and short setup notes. The final submission deadline is Thursday 24 September. Also today, the UN 5-minute demo video deadline is 2:00 PM CAT, and the first METI Open Hour is at 3:00 PM CAT:

https://teams.microsoft.com/l/meetup-join/19%3ameeting_MjlkNWYyMjYtMGNhMi00NDM1LTlkNmYtOTZhYTU2MDU4MDc2%40thread.v2/0?context=%7B%22Tid%22%3A%22b3e5db5e-2944-4837-99f5-7488ace54319%22%2C%22Oid%22%3A%2225f213f2-0e2f-4763-83fa-0d909a0e9701%22%7D

On Tuesday 22 September at 3:00 PM CAT, the Wadhwani class will focus on identifying and validating customers: who the real customer is, who benefits, and who will pay. Thursday 24 September at 3:00 PM CAT is the weekly Q&A/coaching session.

There are also METI Open Hours on Monday with @Gift Cohot 1 Timbukutu and Wednesday with @~Diane at 3:00 PM CAT.

METI Open Hour  
The first METI “Ask Us Anything” Open Hour took place today at 3:00 PM CAT / 2:00 PM WAT. It was an optional space to ask questions about your business or challenges. Join link: https://teams.microsoft.com/l/meetup-join/19%3ameeting_MjlkNWYyMjYtMGNhMi00NDM1LTlkNmYtOTZhYTU2MDU4MDc2%40thread.v2/0?context=%7B%22Tid%22%3A%22b3e5db5e-2944-4837-99f5-7488ace54319%22%2C%22Oid%22%3A%2225f213f2-0e2f-4763-83fa-0d909a0e9701%22%7D

Regular Open Hours will continue every Monday with @Gift Cohot 1 Timbukutu and every Wednesday with @~Diane. Links for those sessions will be shared separately.

UN video demo deadline  
The deadline to submit the 5-minute UN video demo was today at 2:00 PM CAT. Submissions had to be emailed to unipods.regional@undp.org, with the video uploaded to Google Drive or WeTransfer and set to “Anyone with the link can view.”

The file should be named Country_SolutionName_YourName and the email should include your full name, country, UniPod, solution name, one-line description, email address and phone number.

[9/19/26, 9:00:29 PM] +229 01 49 48 62 56: Once everyone has enrolled individually on the Wadhwani platform under the same country cohort:

Open your dashboard and click “Create my venture” at the top right. Enter your business name, choose the closest industry, add a short description, select your country and nearest city, then save.

Only one person per team should create the venture. That person can then use “Add member” to search for and add teammates. The whole team will share the same venture dashboard and slides, so coordinate before editing. Teams should have no more than 5 members.

Bonjour, je suis UniConnect-BOT, développé par l'équipe UniConnect.

J'ai lu tout ce qui s'est dit dans ce groupe, ainsi que le brief du hackathon et le guide vidéo. Deux façons de me parler :

- Dans le groupe : écris @ask puis ta question. Je ne réponds que si on m’appelle, jamais autrement.
- En privé : écris moi directement, sans @ask, et personne d’autre ne le voit. C’est là que je sers le plus.

Voici ce que je sais de ce groupe.

- - -

What this group is

This is the WhatsApp group for Cohort 1 of the UNDP timbuktoo UniPods METI AI Innovation Programme. It brings together 300+ selected innovators from across Africa (chosen from 670 applicants across 24 countries) who are doing AI and entrepreneurship training together. It is run by UNDP staff who post session links, deadlines and documents here.

What has been happening

- The programme has four tracks: MIT Universal AI (self-paced online course), Wadhwani Ignite (14-week entrepreneurship programme with live sessions), later advanced training with the Ethiopian AI Institute, and an in-person bootcamp in Addis Ababa for selected teams.
- MIT course invitations went out by email around 15 September. Only the person whose email applied is enrolled, and the certificate is in that person's name. Team mates cannot be added; the enrolled person may share their login. Many people had trouble finding the course: use the exact enrolment link from the email, then click Dashboard, not Home.
- Wadhwani Ignite started 15 September. Early confusion about an email calling it "Ignite" was resolved: Wadhwani's own system is also called Ignite, so the emails were correct. Charles Bolton runs those sessions; there is a separate Wadhwani WhatsApp group, link sent by email.
- A chatbot hackathon was launched, and it has dominated the group. It came out of complaints that the chat is too busy and people miss things. Gift proposed it, Diane published the guidelines. $5,000 prize, one winning team, judged by a vote of the whole group.
- Enormous team-formation activity: people posting skills, countries and gender, because each team needs at least one woman and cannot be all from one country. A group poll (41 to 5) allowed two members from the same country.
- Bots being tested one team per day in the group, scheduled by Diane. Shadrak's bot (meti_bot / PodPal) went first on 18-19 September and answered hundreds of questions; Nexus was next. Other teams named include AskBack AI Bot, Swift Agents, Wise-Bot.
- A UN demo video call: one innovator's 5-minute video to be shown at a UNGA side event in New York. Guidelines were shared as a document.
- A team/business-name Google Sheet had to be reissued after someone edited the headers.
- Side threads: several smaller WhatsApp groups by sector (health, agritech, edtech), a UniPods TikTok account, and an optional "Pitch Arena" group.

Dates that matter

- 8 September (past): first call / Q&A.
- 10 September (past): Welcome and Orientation session.
- 15 September (past): programme start; Wadhwani Module 1; MIT invitation emails arrived.
- 16 September (past): MIT Universal AI welcome and onboarding call.
- 16 September (past): deadline to fill in your row on the team/business sheet was Wednesday 16 September.
- 17 September (past): hackathon team declarations due, close of business, by email.
- 18 September, 2:00 PM CAT (past): UN demo video submission deadline.
- 18 September (past): first METI Open Hour "Ask Us Anything".
- 18 to 24 September: the hackathon runs. Submission deadline Thursday 24 September.
- 20 September (past): the UNGA "Building the Workforce of the Future" event in New York.
- 18 October 2026: expected completion date for the MIT Universal AI course to stay eligible.
- Week of 23 November 2026: Ethiopian AI Institute selects the 50 strongest projects.
- 1 December 2026: Addis Ababa bootcamp begins, one person per selected team.
- February 2027: second bootcamp for teams not selected.
- Recurring: Wadhwani live sessions Tuesdays and Thursdays; Open Hours Mondays with Gift and Wednesdays with Diane.

Who does what

- Diane: main programme coordinator and admin. Posts links, sheets, guidelines, adds members, schedules bot testing. Ask her by DM or by email.
- Gift Ntuli: programme lead. Ran the first call, proposed the hackathon, answers policy questions.
- Munira: MIT Universal AI contact.
- Jeovaire Umukundwa: part of the UNDP support team.
- Charles Bolton: Wadhwani Ignite facilitator, runs the platform sessions.
- Shadrak (Benin): built the first working hackathon bot, deployed in the group.

Things you keep needing

- Programme email for everything: unipods.regional@undp.org
- MIT Universal AI support: uaisupport@mit.edu
- MIT course page: https://learn.mit.edu/universal-learning/ai
- Wadhwani Ignite signup: https://web.nen.wfglobal.org/en/login?mode=createAccount&source=student
- Team/business name sheet: https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/15sAD53FA9LZXJ7EzOIzWLALTViaPz2_e/edit
- Wadhwani session Teams link: https://teams.microsoft.com/meet/419860837373470?p=jYchWkDZnC4etsclnK (ID 419 860 837 373 470, passcode g2Z7gc7Q)
- MIT session Teams link: https://teams.microsoft.com/meet/369123389215172?p=E4YbRFyzKjPR5AFYUf
- Open Hour Teams link: https://teams.microsoft.com/l/meetup-join/19%3ameeting_MjlkNWYyMjYtMGNhMi00NDM1LTlkNmYtOTZhYTU2MDU4MDc2%40thread.v2/0?context=%7B%22Tid%22%3A%22b3e5db5e-2944-4837-99f5-7488ace54319%22%2C%22Oid%22%3A%2225f213f2-0e2f-4763-83fa-0d909a0e9701%22%7D
- Recordings: MIT onboarding https://drive.google.com/file/d/1E5RrwULX8zSjwxHFSxiQzCTtp20ulYQ8/view ; Wadhwani Welcome + Module 0 https://youtu.be/yVji4ZQECVw ; Module 1 class https://youtu.be/6q4uPBO_sDc ; Module 1 coaching/Q&A https://youtu.be/-6G7LXiu47o

Still open

- How judging of the hackathon will actually work with many bots live at once. More info was promised and never posted. Testing slots were still being booked as late as 28 September, past the stated 24 September deadline.
- Whether late team declarations, formed after 17 September, will be accepted.
- Whether a team may have three members from the same country. The document says only that members cannot all be from the same country; the two-per-country line came from a group poll, not an admin ruling.
- Whether the UN video deadline was extended for people with upload problems. Asked, never answered.
- Whether every team member must complete the courses or only the enrolled founder.
- Details of the UniPod end-of-month meeting and transport expenses. Asked, never answered.
- Most country UniPods had not contacted participants as of 18 September; Gift said contact would come the following week.
- Several people complained the group is too fast for French speakers and that information is being missed.

[9/21/26, 2:31:27 PM] +234 803 766 9735: privacy
[9/21/26, 2:31:27 PM] +234 701 874 6343: Privacy
[9/21/26, 2:31:29 PM] +251 97 055 5577: Privacy

- I store messages of this group (text, captions, voice-note transcripts) in the programme's own database so I can answer questions and write summaries. Raw messages are kept for 365 days.
- From them I keep short Q&A notes (e.g. "When is demo day? - Friday") and dated calendar items (sessions, deadlines, announcements). Admins can remove any of them (faq remove, event remove, wrong).
- Messages deleted "for everyone" are deleted from my memory too.
- Private chats with me are not stored; I only remember the last few turns for 30 minutes.
- I only message you privately after you have written to me first. I note that you did, and keep answers waiting for you (for up to 7 days) until you do.
- Text is sent to the configured AI provider to generate answers. I never share one group's content with people outside that group.

[9/21/26, 2:33:50 PM] +251 97 055 5577: Help — METI UniPods AI Innovation Programme, Cohort 1

ASK ME ANYTHING

- Mention me with your question
  I search the group, the programme pack, pinned FAQs
  and meeting minutes, then answer you privately — with
  who said it and when.
  "When is the workplan due?"
  "Who is our Wadhwani facilitator?"

- If it was already answered, I send you that answer.
  If not, I ask the admins and forward their reply.

PROGRAMME

- today — sessions, deadlines and news for today
- upcoming — next sessions, trainings and UniPod meetups
- deadlines — every date ahead, with days remaining
- announcements — newest first (announcements 30 = last 30 days)
- tracks — where MIT, Wadhwani, the Institute and the bootcamp stand

CATCHING UP

- catchup — what you missed since your last message
- summary or summary 12 — last 24 hours, or last 12
- You can also just ask: "I need a recap of this week
  sessions", "can you summarize today?"
- meeting <title> — caption or reply to a recording and I
  post minutes. meetings lists them.

ADMINS

- faq add <question> || <answer> · faq remove <number>
- wrong — reply to an answer of mine to make me forget it
- autoanswer on / off · digest set <hours> · digest off
- delete — reply to a message
- announce <text> — post a programme announcement
- import — send a chat export .txt with this caption 'import' to
  load history from before I joined

Reply to any unanswered question I forward you.

I keep track of this group's discussions so nobody has to scroll through hundreds of messages:
- I answer questions that were already answered here, automatically and privately, so this chat stays readable
- today and upcoming show what's on, what's due and what's next
- catchup sends you a private summary of what you missed
- share a meeting recording or transcript with the caption meeting <title> and I'll post minutes
- mention me with any question, anytime

Messages in this group are stored to power these features. Type privacy for details and help for all commands.

[9/21/26, 4:08:33 PM] +251 97 055 5577: Help — METI UniPods AI Innovation Programme, Cohort 1

ASK ME ANYTHING

- Mention me with your question
  I search the group, the programme pack, pinned FAQs
  and meeting minutes, then answer you privately — with
  who said it and when.
  "When is the workplan due?"
  "Who is our Wadhwani facilitator?"

- If it was already answered, I send you that answer.
  If not, I ask the admins and forward their reply.

PROGRAMME

- today — sessions, deadlines and news for today
- upcoming — next sessions, trainings and UniPod meetups
- deadlines — every date ahead, with days remaining
- announcements — newest first (announcements 30 = last 30 days)
- tracks — where MIT, Wadhwani, the Institute and the bootcamp stand

CATCHING UP

- catchup — what you missed since your last message
- summary or summary 12 — last 24 hours, or last 12
- You can also just ask: "I need a recap of this week
  sessions", "can you summarize today?"
- meeting <title> — caption or reply to a recording and I
  post minutes. meetings lists them.

ADMINS

- faq add <question> || <answer> · faq remove <number>
- wrong — reply to an answer of mine to make me forget it
- autoanswer on / off · digest set <hours> · digest off
- delete — reply to a message
- announce <text> — post a programme announcement
- import — send a chat export .txt with this caption 'import' to
  load history from before I joined

Reply to any unanswered question I forward you.

[9/21/26, 4:14:40 PM] +251 97 055 5577: Here are some motivations to help you focus on the MIT course:

- Set clear goals: Define what you want to achieve by completing the course, such as skills you want to gain or projects you want to work on.
- Create a study schedule: Allocate specific times each day for course work to build a routine.
- Join study groups: Collaborate with peers to share insights and keep each other accountable.
- Reward yourself: Plan small rewards for completing modules or reaching milestones to stay motivated.
- Visualize success: Imagine how completing the course will benefit your career and projects, keeping your end goals in mind.

These strategies can help maintain your focus and ensure timely completion.

Bonjour, je suis UniConnect-BOT, développé par l'équipe UniConnect.

J'ai lu tout ce qui s'est dit dans ce groupe, ainsi que le brief du hackathon et le guide vidéo. Deux façons de me parler :

- Dans le groupe : écris @ask puis ta question. Je ne réponds que si on m’appelle, jamais autrement.
- En privé : écris moi directement, sans @ask, et personne d’autre ne le voit. C’est là que je sers le plus.

Voici ce que je sais de ce groupe.

- - -

What this group is

This is the WhatsApp group for Cohort 1 of the UNDP timbuktoo UniPods METI AI Innovation Programme. It brings together 300+ selected innovators from across Africa (chosen from 670 applicants across 24 countries) who are doing AI and entrepreneurship training together. It is run by UNDP staff who post session links, deadlines and documents here.

What has been happening

- The programme has four tracks: MIT Universal AI (self-paced online course), Wadhwani Ignite (14-week entrepreneurship programme with live sessions), later advanced training with the Ethiopian AI Institute, and an in-person bootcamp in Addis Ababa for selected teams.
- MIT course invitations went out by email around 15 September. Only the person whose email applied is enrolled, and the certificate is in that person's name. Team mates cannot be added; the enrolled person may share their login. Many people had trouble finding the course: use the exact enrolment link from the email, then click Dashboard, not Home.
- Wadhwani Ignite started 15 September. Early confusion about an email calling it "Ignite" was resolved: Wadhwani's own system is also called Ignite, so the emails were correct. Charles Bolton runs those sessions; there is a separate Wadhwani WhatsApp group, link sent by email.
- A chatbot hackathon was launched, and it has dominated the group. It came out of complaints that the chat is too busy and people miss things. Gift proposed it, Diane published the guidelines. $5,000 prize, one winning team, judged by a vote of the whole group.
- Enormous team-formation activity: people posting skills, countries and gender, because each team needs at least one woman and cannot be all from one country. A group poll (41 to 5) allowed two members from the same country.
- Bots being tested one team per day in the group, scheduled by Diane. Shadrak's bot (meti_bot / PodPal) went first on 18-19 September and answered hundreds of questions; Nexus was next. Other teams named include AskBack AI Bot, Swift Agents, Wise-Bot.
- A UN demo video call: one innovator's 5-minute video to be shown at a UNGA side event in New York. Guidelines were shared as a document.
- A team/business-name Google Sheet had to be reissued after someone edited the headers.
- Side threads: several smaller WhatsApp groups by sector (health, agritech, edtech), a UniPods TikTok account, and an optional "Pitch Arena" group.

Dates that matter

- 8 September (past): first call / Q&A.
- 10 September (past): Welcome and Orientation session.
- 15 September (past): programme start; Wadhwani Module 1; MIT invitation emails arrived.
- 16 September (past): MIT Universal AI welcome and onboarding call.
- 16 September (past): deadline to fill in your row on the team/business sheet was Wednesday 16 September.
- 17 September (past): hackathon team declarations due, close of business, by email.
- 18 September, 2:00 PM CAT (past): UN demo video submission deadline.
- 18 September (past): first METI Open Hour "Ask Us Anything".
- 18 to 24 September: the hackathon runs. Submission deadline Thursday 24 September.
- 20 September (past): the UNGA "Building the Workforce of the Future" event in New York.
- 18 October 2026: expected completion date for the MIT Universal AI course to stay eligible.
- Week of 23 November 2026: Ethiopian AI Institute selects the 50 strongest projects.
- 1 December 2026: Addis Ababa bootcamp begins, one person per selected team.
- February 2027: second bootcamp for teams not selected.
- Recurring: Wadhwani live sessions Tuesdays and Thursdays; Open Hours Mondays with Gift and Wednesdays with Diane.

Who does what

- Diane: main programme coordinator and admin. Posts links, sheets, guidelines, adds members, schedules bot testing. Ask her by DM or by email.
- Gift Ntuli: programme lead. Ran the first call, proposed the hackathon, answers policy questions.
- Munira: MIT Universal AI contact.
- Jeovaire Umukundwa: part of the UNDP support team.
- Charles Bolton: Wadhwani Ignite facilitator, runs the platform sessions.
- Shadrak (Benin): built the first working hackathon bot, deployed in the group.

How it works, when our day comes:

[9/22/26, 2:09:46 PM] +251 90 260 1557: [Forwarded] Here is what I currently know about the UniPods METI AI Programme 2026 and its hackathon:

Programme and learning
- The MIT Universal AI course is self-paced.
- Expected completion date: 18 October 2026.
- MIT course:
https://learn.mit.edu/universal-learning/ai
- Wadhwani Ignite is a 14-week entrepreneurship programme with live sessions every Tuesday and Thursday.
- Next Wadhwani class: Tuesday, 22 September at 3:00 PM CAT.
- Next Q&A/coaching session: Thursday, 24 September at 3:00 PM CAT.
- Wadhwani sign-up:
https://web.nen.wfglobal.org/en/login?mode=createAccount&source=student
- Ethiopian AI Institute:
https://aii.et/

- Nexus is the UniPods METI AI programme assistant bot.

Hackathon
- The challenge is to build a practical, working chatbot that solves a real communication problem.
- A slide deck, idea, or mock-up alone is not enough.
- The chatbot may be delivered through:
- WhatsApp
- Telegram
- A web interface
- There is no single required platform.
- The submission must include:
- A working chatbot
- Access to the source code or repository
- Short setup instructions
- Bots should be named using the team name followed by “BOT”.
- Teams must inform Diane before deploying a bot. Bots should not be deployed randomly.
- Testing slots are booked up to 3 October. Testers act as judges and record scores while testing the bots.
- Hackathon testing and project work run through 24 September 2026.
- The submission deadline is Thursday, 24 September 2026.
- The separate 5-minute UN video-demo deadline on 18 September at 2:00 PM CAT has already passed.

Team rules
- A team can have a maximum of 5 members.
- Each team must include at least one woman.
- Team members should come from different countries.
- The reported rule allows up to 2 members from the same country.
- A solo founder can participate by writing “Solo” in the team sheet.
- Team declarations were due at the end of business on 17 September 2026.
- Late declarations may still be emailed, but confirmation should be obtained from Diane.
- To declare a team, email unipods.regional@undp.org.
- Use the subject: *UniPods Hackathon
- Team Declaration*.
- Include the team name, all members, and their countries.
- There is no separate declaration link.

Registration
- Complete only your solution’s row in the registration sheet.
- Add the business name and team members.
- For a solo founder, write “Solo”.
- Registration sheet:
https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/15sAD53FA9LZXJ7EzOIzWLALTViaPz2_e/edit

Selection of the 50 projects
- The 50 projects are selected from teams that:
- Complete the MIT Universal AI course
- Stay up to date with the Wadhwani programme
- Selected teams work with the Ethiopian AI Institute to create a tailored workplan.
- In the last week of November 2026, the Institute selects the 50 strongest solutions for the Addis Ababa bootcamp.
- Each selected team sends one participant.
- Teams not selected for the November bootcamp may be considered for the second bootcamp in February 2027.

Programme dates and sessions
- The broader UniPods Hackathon week is listed as 17–24 September 2026.
- The chatbot hackathon itself is listed as 18–24 September 2026.
- The first METI Open Hour took place on 18 September at 3:00 PM CAT.
- The next Wadhwani class is 22 September at 3:00 PM CAT.
- The next coaching/Q&A session is 24 September at 3:00 PM CAT.

Available recordings
- MIT Universal AI welcome and onboarding, 16 September:
https://drive.google.com/file/d/1E5RrwULX8zSjwxHFSxiQzCTtp20ulYQ8/view
- Wadhwani Welcome and Module 0, 10 September:
https://youtu.be/yVji4ZQECVw

https://youtu.be/6q4uPBO_sDc

https://youtu.be/-6G7LXiu47o

Contacts
- Hackathon and team declarations: unipods.regional@undp.org
- Hackathon coordinator for team declarations and bot deployment: Diane

[9/22/26, 7:35:24 PM] +229 97 42 65 40: @ask 
Why do you all use group messages as a knowledge base?  😁😂
[9/22/26, 7:35:32 PM] +223 74 42 77 59: Because that is the hackathon problem statement: the group grew large, high chat traffic means people miss messages and re-ask questions already addressed, people miss meetings and cannot rewatch call recordings, so information gets lost across chats and calls. The challenge is to build a chatbot that makes sense of all that data, calls, chats and more, and answers people directly.

One caveat raised in the group: a bot that picks up random unnecessary conversations and stores them is unfit, because the programme team would pay a lot of API cost for irrelevant information in the knowledge base.

- Akpan, Victor D., 22 Sept · METI UniPods Hackathon brief
